I have complete context. Here is my adversarial security review.

## SECURITY REVIEW COMPLETE

**Files analyzed:** 5 (`client.ts`, `folder-tree.ts`, `types.ts`, `ecies/decrypt.ts`, plus `core/folder/types.ts`, `crypto/utils/encoding.ts` for verification)
**Crypto operations found:** 2 ECIES unwraps per subfolder in DFS (`folderKey`, `ipnsPrivateKey`), key copy/zeroize lifecycle
**Issues found:** 0 CRITICAL, 0 HIGH, 1 MEDIUM, 2 LOW, 2 INFO

I found no real exploitable vulnerability in the change. The six concerns raised in the brief all check out as correct. Detailed findings below, by severity.

---

### Assessment of each focus area

**1. KEY ZEROIZATION / MEMORY HYGIENE — Correct, not a new leak.**

The decision to NOT zero the transient `folderKey`/`ipnsPrivateKey` buffers in `ensureFolderLoaded` (`client.ts:462-474`) is correct. `loadFolder` builds the returned `state` holding those buffers *by reference* (`client.ts:375-383`), while `folderTree.set` stores a *clone* (`folder-tree.ts:29-38`). The target `childState` is returned to the caller (e.g. bin restore) which then uses `folder.folderKey` / `folder.ipnsKeypair.privateKey` to publish. Zeroing the transient buffers after `loadFolder` would corrupt the returned `FolderState` (the buffers are the same objects), breaking the caller. So eager zeroing is *infeasible here by design*, not an oversight.

Critically, this is NOT a new leak class. The pre-existing `loadFolder` (public method) and `registerFolder` (`client.ts:304-324`) already accept plaintext `folderKey`/`ipnsKeypair` and leave the caller-passed transient inputs to GC — `registerFolder` clones into the tree and the caller's originals are never zeroed by the SDK. `ensureFolderLoaded` moves the unwrap inside the SDK but the residual-plaintext lifetime is identical: ephemeral buffers handed to `loadFolder`, cloned into the tree, originals left to GC. Same hygiene posture as the code already shipped. No regression.

`destroy()` correctly zeroes `internalRootIpnsKeypair` (both private and public, `client.ts:249-252`), guarded by the null check. Confirmed.

**2. KEY MATERIAL HANDLING — Consistent with vaultKeypair/rootFolderKey.**

`rootIpnsKeypair` follows the established pattern exactly:
- Defensive deep copy in constructor (`client.ts:76-81`), guarded by `if (config.rootIpnsKeypair)`.
- Re-pointed into `this.config` via conditional spread (`client.ts:86`) so `this.config.rootIpnsKeypair` aliases the internal copy, never the caller buffer.
- Zeroed in `destroy()` (`client.ts:249-252`).

No path zeroes a caller buffer: the only `.fill(0)` calls on the IPNS keypair are on `internalRootIpnsKeypair` (the copy). `ensureFolderLoaded` passes `this.internalRootIpnsKeypair` to `loadFolder` (`client.ts:436`), which hands it to `folderTree.set` — and `set` *clones* it (`folder-tree.ts:33-36`), so the root keypair stored in the tree is a third-generation copy. When `folderTree.clear()` zeroes that tree copy on destroy, it does NOT touch `internalRootIpnsKeypair`, and `destroy()` zeroes `internalRootIpnsKeypair` separately. No double-free hazard, no caller-buffer corruption, no un-zeroed internal copy.

**3. WRONG-KEY / CONFUSED-DEPUTY — No transposition.**

Field mapping is correct (`client.ts:462-469` against `core/folder/types.ts:39-42`):
- `entry.folderKeyEncrypted` → unwrapped → `folderKey` → passed as the `folderKey` arg to `loadFolder` (used as AES-256 metadata key). Correct.
- `entry.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted` → unwrapped → `ipnsPrivateKey` → passed as `privateKey` in the keypair (used as Ed25519 signing key). Correct.

No transposition, no parent-key reuse: each child's own `entry.*Encrypted` fields are unwrapped fresh per iteration. The DFS pushes each loaded `childState` (with its own keys) onto the stack; the parent's `folderKey` is never reused for a child. Both unwraps use `this.internalVaultKeypair.privateKey` as the ECIES recipient key, which is correct since all subfolder keys are wrapped to the user's vault public key. Confirmed.

**4. CYCLES / DOS — Bounded, but see MEDIUM below.**

The `visited` Set (`client.ts:445`) is checked AND populated before any unwrap/load (`client.ts:452-453`), so each `ipnsName` triggers at most one resolve+2 unwraps. A cycle (folder A lists B, B lists A) terminates: A's entry is in `visited` from the seed or first visit. No infinite loop. Early-termination on match (`client.ts:478`) is correct — it returns the freshly loaded `childState`, not a stale lookup. Failed resolves (`childState === null`) are skipped, not retried (`client.ts:477`).

The bound is "every folder reachable from root," which is the legitimate tree size. This is acceptable for the intended single-user vault, but note the MEDIUM finding on worst-case amplification.

**5. ERROR LEAKAGE — No key bytes exposed.**

`unwrapKey` throws a generic `CryptoError('Key unwrapping failed', ...)` with no key bytes (`ecies/decrypt.ts:46-53`). `hexToBytes` throws `'Invalid hex string'` with no secret content. `withOperation` re-emits the caught error object via `onError` and the `error` event (`client.ts:2024-2027`) but does not stringify/log key bytes — and the errors on this path carry none. No `console.log` of keys anywhere on the path. Confirmed clean.

**6. BACKWARD-COMPAT SAFETY — Preserved.**

`ensureFolderLoaded` returns `null` at `client.ts:426` (`if (!this.internalRootIpnsKeypair) return null`) *before any crypto*, after only the cheap `folderTree.get` short-circuit. The throwers use `?? await this.ensureFolderLoaded(...)` then `if (!folder) throw new Error('Folder not loaded')` (e.g. `client.ts:603-605`, `733-735`, `803-804`), so when `rootIpnsKeypair` is unconfigured the exact prior `'Folder not loaded'` behavior is preserved. Confirmed.

---

### MEDIUM

#### [MEDIUM] DFS re-walks the entire reachable tree on every cache miss for an unreachable/not-yet-published target

**Location:** `client.ts:447-484`

**Issue:** When the target is NOT reachable from root (e.g. a folder whose parent metadata hasn't propagated yet via IPNS, a deleted folder still referenced by a stale caller, or a target IPNS name that legitimately isn't under root), the loop exhausts the full stack and returns `null` only after resolving + unwrapping *every* folder in the vault. Because there is no negative cache, a caller that repeatedly attempts an operation on an unresolvable folder (a retry loop in the web/FUSE tier) re-walks and re-resolves the whole tree each time — N IPNS resolves and 2N ECIES unwraps per attempt.

**Impact:** Self-inflicted DoS / CPU + network amplification under retry, not attacker-controlled key compromise. An untrusted server that withholds the target subfolder's IPNS record (so it never resolves) can also force every `ensureFolderLoaded(target)` call to do maximum work, since the target is never found and the walk never short-circuits. Severity is bounded by vault size and caller retry policy, hence MEDIUM not HIGH.

**Recommendation:** This is a robustness concern, not a correctness bug — acceptable to ship if callers don't tight-loop on unresolvable targets. If retry loops exist upstream, add a short-lived negative-result memo (per `targetIpnsName`) or cap the number of folders walked per call. No code change required for security correctness; flag for the consumer-tier retry review.

---

### LOW

#### [LOW] Tree-stored root IPNS keypair carries a zero-length publicKey placeholder for subfolders

**Location:** `client.ts:471-474`

**Issue:** Subfolders are loaded with `publicKey: new Uint8Array(0)` (signing derives the public key from the private key later). This is intentional and documented, and `folder-tree.set` clones the empty array harmlessly. Not a vulnerability — flagged only so reviewers don't mistake the empty `publicKey` for a bug, and to note any future consumer that reads `ipnsKeypair.publicKey` from a bootstrapped subfolder will get an empty array rather than the real key.

**Recommendation:** No action needed for security. If any code path reads `publicKey` off a bootstrapped subfolder state, it must derive it from the private key, consistent with the comment.

#### [LOW] Transient unwrapped buffers on the *unreachable-target* path are never referenced again but also never zeroed

**Location:** `client.ts:462-474`

**Issue:** For subfolders that get walked but are NOT the target, the unwrapped `folderKey`/`ipnsPrivateKey` are cloned into `folderTree` (zeroed on destroy) and the originals are left to GC. Same as the existing `registerFolder`/`loadFolder` hygiene, so consistent — but worth noting these plaintext buffers persist until GC for every folder in the vault on a full walk, widening the transient plaintext footprint vs. a single `loadFolder` call. This is inherent to bootstrapping, not a new leak *class*, but it is a larger *quantity* of simultaneously-live plaintext than any pre-existing single call.

**Recommendation:** Acceptable given the by-reference return constraint (you can't zero the target's buffers, and zeroing only the non-target ones adds complexity for marginal benefit since clones already exist in the tree unencrypted anyway). No change required; documented for completeness.

---

### INFO

#### [INFO] `getConfig()` exposes the internal root IPNS private key by reference

**Location:** `client.ts:339-341` (`getConfig` returns `this.config`, whose `rootIpnsKeypair` now aliases `internalRootIpnsKeypair`)

**Issue:** `getConfig()` returns `this.config` directly. With the new field, a consumer calling `getConfig().rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey` gets a live reference to the internal root signing key (and could mutate/zero it, corrupting the client). This is pre-existing behavior for `vaultKeypair`/`rootFolderKey` (also aliased in `this.config`), so the new field is *consistent* with the existing exposure surface — not a regression. Flagged because the root IPNS key is the highest-value signing key in the hierarchy and `getConfig` is `@internal` but not access-controlled.

**Recommendation:** No change for this PR (consistent with existing keys). If hardening later, `getConfig()` should return a shallow copy that omits or clones key material.

#### [INFO] Corrupt/malformed `*Encrypted` hex in folder metadata aborts the whole walk

**Location:** `client.ts:462-469` via `hexToBytes` (`crypto/utils/encoding.ts:14`)

**Issue:** If a subfolder entry has a malformed `folderKeyEncrypted`/`ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted` (odd length / non-hex), `hexToBytes` throws and the throw propagates out of `ensureFolderLoaded` (uncaught by the loop), aborting the entire bootstrap and surfacing as the operation's error — rather than skipping that one bad subfolder and continuing the DFS. The error message is generic (no key bytes), so no leakage. This is a resilience/availability nuance: one corrupt sibling entry blocks reaching unrelated targets.

**Recommendation:** Optional — wrap the per-child unwrap in try/catch and `continue` on failure (mirroring the `if (!childState) continue` skip) so a single corrupt entry doesn't deny access to the rest of the tree. Not a security defect; the server cannot use this to leak keys, only to deny availability of unrelated folders (which an untrusted server can already do by withholding records).

---

### Bottom line

The change is **security-correct**. Key zeroization is handled consistently with the established `vaultKeypair`/`rootFolderKey` pattern; `destroy()` zeroes the new internal copy; no caller buffers are zeroed unexpectedly; no key transposition or confused-deputy in the DFS; cycles are bounded by `visited`; errors leak no key bytes; and backward-compat returns `null` before any crypto when `rootIpnsKeypair` is absent. The only substantive finding is the MEDIUM amplification-under-retry / server-withholding DoS, which is an availability/robustness concern bounded by vault size — not a key-compromise or zero-knowledge-model violation. I did not invent issues beyond these.

Relevant files:
- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/client.ts` (method at lines 421-485; constructor copy 71-87; destroy 240-254; throwers e.g. 603-605, 733-735, 803-804; `getConfig` 339-341)
- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/state/folder-tree.ts` (clone-on-set 29-38; zeroize 41-66)
- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/packages/crypto/src/ecies/decrypt.ts` (generic-error unwrap 46-53)
- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/packages/core/src/folder/types.ts` (FolderEntry field semantics 31-47)

{
  "command": "cd /Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box\ngit add apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts packages/sdk/src/client.ts packages/sdk/src/types.ts packages/sdk/src/__tests__/ensure-folder-loaded.test.ts tests/web-e2e/tests/bin-restore-after-reload.spec.ts\ngit commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(sdk): self-bootstrap folder tree from root IPNS key\n\nThe SDK client owned folder state (folderTree) but its config carried only\nrootIpnsName + rootFolderKey, not the root IPNS signing key. So the client\ncould not resolve/publish root or lazy-load folders on its own, and the web\napp had to seed folderTree via ensureFolderRegistered before every\nfolderTree-dependent method. That asymmetry is the root cause of the whole\n\"Folder not loaded\" class — most visibly, restoring a recycle-bin item after\na reload threw 'Target folder not loaded' when the item's subfolder parent was\nnever navigated to that session (pre-existing since #296; recycle-bin.spec.ts\nmisses it because it uploads in-session and never reloads).\n\nChanges:\n- Add optional rootIpnsKeypair to CipherBoxClientConfig; defensively copied in\n  the constructor and zeroed in destroy(), mirroring vaultKeypair/rootFolderKey.\n- Add CipherBoxClient.ensureFolderLoaded(target): when a root IPNS keypair is\n  configured, walk the folder tree from root (DFS, early exit), unwrapping each\n  subfolder's folderKeyEncrypted/ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted with the vault keypair\n  and loading metadata until the target is registered. Every folder on the path\n  is cached. A corrupt sibling entry is skipped, not fatal.\n- Wire ensureFolderLoaded as a self-heal fallback into every folderTree-dependent\n  method (create/rename/move/delete/upload(s)/replace/version ops/share) and the\n  bin deleteToBin/restoreFromBin wrappers. When no rootIpnsKeypair is configured\n  it returns null before any crypto, preserving the prior 'Folder not loaded'\n  behavior.\n- Web: pass vaultStore.rootIpnsKeypair into the SDK config at initSdkClient\n  (already guaranteed non-null by the existing guard).\n\nSecurity: no new key exposure — the client already holds vaultKeypair.privateKey,\nrootFolderKey, and every loaded folder's ipnsPrivateKey in memory (zeroed on\ndestroy). Reviewed: no key transposition, cycles bounded by a visited set,\nerrors leak no key bytes.\n\nTests:\n- SDK unit: ensureFolderLoaded — already-loaded no-op, no-keypair null,\n  root-as-target, deep walk, unreachable target, skip unresolvable/corrupt sibling.\n- Web E2E: restore a subfolder item after reload without navigating into the\n  subfolder (the scenario recycle-bin.spec.ts misses).\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\necho \"=== committed ===\" && git log --oneline -2",
  "description": "Commit the implementation"
}

> AGENT

Committed on `feat/sdk-client-self-bootstrap-folder-tree` (working tree clean). Here's where things stand:

## Done & verified

**Implementation** (`packages/sdk` + `apps/web`)
- `CipherBoxClientConfig.rootIpnsKeypair` added — defensive-copied in the constructor, zeroed in `destroy()` (mirrors `vaultKeypair`/`rootFolderKey`).
- `CipherBoxClient.ensureFolderLoaded(target)` — DFS-walks from root, unwrapping each subfolder's `folderKeyEncrypted`/`ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted` with the vault keypair and loading metadata until the target is registered; caches the whole path; skips a corrupt sibling instead of aborting.
- Wired as a self-heal fallback into **every** folderTree-dependent method (create/rename/move/delete/upload(s)/replace/version ops/share) + the bin `deleteToBin`/`restoreFromBin` wrappers — via `?? await this.ensureFolderLoaded(x)`, so it returns `null` before any crypto when no `rootIpnsKeypair` is configured (prior `'Folder not loaded'` behavior preserved).
- Web: `initSdkClient` now passes `vaultStore.rootIpnsKeypair` (already non-null by the existing guard).

**Verification**
- `pnpm typecheck` clean (rebuilds the dep chain + web `tsc -b`).
- SDK suite: **179 passing** (3 live-API integration skipped under CI), incl. a new 7-case `ensure-folder-loaded.test.ts`.
- New web-E2E `bin-restore-after-reload.spec.ts` (subfolder restore after reload — the gap `recycle-bin.spec.ts` misses) typechecks + lints. Note: web-e2e runs only on main-push, not PRs.
- ESLint clean. Adversarial **security review: 0 critical / 0 high** — key handling correct, no transposition, cycles bounded, no key-byte leakage.

**Scope note:** I kept the web `ensureFolderRegistered` calls — they're now redundant no-ops (SDK self-heals) but harmless. Removing those ~16 call sites is a safe follow-up once self-heal proves out; I didn't want to widen this PR's blast radius. The one residual robustness item from review (no negative-cache → an unreachable target re-walks the tree) is a follow-up, not a correctness issue.

Want me to push the branch and open the PR?

27	+// Mock sdk-core: keep everything real except loadFolderMetadata, which we drive

31	+  return {

34	+  };

37	+// Mock crypto: ensureFolderLoaded unwraps each subfolder's keys with these.
38	+// Return value content is irrelevant — loadFolderMetadata is mocked, so the
39	+// "decrypted" key is never actually used to decrypt anything.
40	+vi.mock('@cipherbox/crypto', () => ({
41	+  unwrapKey: vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(new Uint8Array(32).fill(9)),
42	+  hexToBytes: vi.fn().mockReturnValue(new Uint8Array(32)),
43	+  clearBytes: vi.fn(),
44	+}));

47	+
48	+const ROOT = 'k51test'; // matches createTestConfig().rootIpnsName
49	+
50	+function folderEntry(ipnsName: string, name: string): FolderEntry {
51	+  return {
52	+    type: 'folder',
53	+    id: `id-${ipnsName}`,
54	+    name,
55	+    ipnsName,
56	+    ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: 'aa',
57	+    folderKeyEncrypted: 'bb',
58	+    createdAt: 1,
59	+    modifiedAt: 1,
60	+  };
61	+}
62	+
63	+function metadata(children: FolderMetadata['children']): FolderMetadata {
64	+  return { version: 'v2', children };
65	+}
66	+
67	+/** Drive loadFolderMetadata to return canned metadata keyed by IPNS name. */
68	+function mockTree(tree: Record<string, FolderMetadata['children']>) {
69	+  vi.mocked(sdkCore.loadFolderMetadata).mockImplementation(async ({ ipnsName }) => {
70	+    const children = tree[ipnsName];
71	+    if (!children) return null;
72	+    return { metadata: metadata(children), sequenceNumber: 1n, cid: `cid-${ipnsName}` };
73	+  });
74	+}

78	+  privateKey: new Uint8Array(64).fill(8),

261	+   * the target is registered. Every folder visited along the way is cached, so

265	+   * configured) or the target is not reachable from root. Callers fall back to

267	+   * when self-bootstrap is unavailable. This dissolves the "Folder not loaded"
268	+   * failure class that previously required consumers to pre-seed folderTree
269	+   * before every folderTree-dependent operation.

273	+   * @internal
274	+   */
275	+  async ensureFolderLoaded(targetIpnsName: string): Promise<FolderState | null> {
276	+    const existing = this.folderTree.get(targetIpnsName);
277	+    if (existing) return existing;
278	+

281	+

509	+import { test, expect, Browser, BrowserContext, Page } from '@playwright/test';
510	+import type { PrivateKeyAccount } from 'viem/accounts';
511	+import { createTestAccount, setupMockWallet, loginViaWallet } from '../utils/wallet-login-helpers';
512	+import { createTestTextFile, cleanupTestFiles } from '../utils/test-files';
513	+import { deleteAccountViaPage } from '../utils/cleanup-helpers';
514	+import { FileListPage } from '../page-objects/file-browser/file-list.page';
515	+import { UploadZonePage } from '../page-objects/file-browser/upload-zone.page';
516	+import { ContextMenuPage } from '../page-objects/file-browser/context-menu.page';
517	+import { ConfirmDialogPage } from '../page-objects/dialogs/confirm-dialog.page';
518	+import { BinPage } from '../page-objects/pages/bin.page';
519	+import { CreateFolderDialogPage } from '../page-objects/dialogs/create-folder-dialog.page';
520	+
521	+/**
522	+ * Recycle Bin: restore-after-reload regression
523	+ *
524	+ * Reproduces the "Target folder not loaded" bug class: restoring a bin item
525	+ * whose original parent is a SUBFOLDER that was never navigated to in the
526	+ * current session. After a reload only the root folder is re-seeded into the
527	+ * SDK folderTree, so the subfolder's keys are absent — pre-fix, restoreFromBin
528	+ * threw 'Target folder not loaded'.
529	+ *
530	+ * The fix gives the SDK client the root IPNS keypair so it can self-bootstrap:
531	+ * client.restoreFromBin → ensureFolderLoaded walks from root, unwraps the
532	+ * subfolder's keys, and loads it before republishing the parent.
533	+ *
534	+ * The existing recycle-bin.spec.ts misses this because it uploads in-session
535	+ * (parent already loaded) and never reloads. This spec lives on its own with
536	+ * its own login so nothing pre-seeds the subfolder into folderTree.
537	+ */
538	+test.describe.serial('Recycle Bin: restore after reload (subfolder parent)', () => {
539	+  let browser: Browser;
540	+  let context: BrowserContext;
541	+  let page: Page;
542	+
543	+  let fileList: FileListPage;
544	+  let uploadZone: UploadZonePage;
545	+  let contextMenu: ContextMenuPage;
546	+  let confirmDialog: ConfirmDialogPage;
547	+  let binPage: BinPage;
548	+  let createFolderDialog: CreateFolderDialogPage;
549	+
550	+  let account: PrivateKeyAccount;
551	+
552	+  test.beforeAll(async ({ browser: testBrowser }) => {
553	+    test.setTimeout(90_000); // Core Kit init + SIWE can be slow
554	+    browser = testBrowser;
555	+    context = await browser.newContext();
556	+    page = await context.newPage();
557	+
558	+    account = createTestAccount();
559	+    await setupMockWallet(page, account);
560	+
561	+    fileList = new FileListPage(page);
562	+    uploadZone = new UploadZonePage(page);
563	+    contextMenu = new ContextMenuPage(page);
564	+    confirmDialog = new ConfirmDialogPage(page);
565	+    binPage = new BinPage(page);
566	+    createFolderDialog = new CreateFolderDialogPage(page);
567	+
568	+    await loginViaWallet(page, { timeout: 60_000 });
569	+    await page.waitForURL('**/files', { timeout: 60000 });
570	+    await Promise.race([
571	+      fileList.fileListContainer().waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30000 }),
572	+      page.locator('[data-testid="empty-state"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30000 }),
573	+    ]);
574	+  });
575	+
576	+  test.afterAll(async () => {
577	+    cleanupTestFiles();
578	+    if (page) {
579	+      await deleteAccountViaPage(page);
580	+    }
581	+    if (context) {
582	+      await context.close();
583	+    }
584	+  });
585	+
586	+  test('restores a subfolder item after reload without navigating into the subfolder', async () => {
587	+    test.slow(); // folder create + upload + delete + reload + restore IPNS cycles
588	+
589	+    const runId = Date.now().toString();
590	+    const subfolderName = `reload-sub-${runId}`;
591	+    const fileName = `reload-restore-${runId}.txt`;
592	+
593	+    // 1. Create a subfolder under root.
594	+    await page.locator('.file-browser-new-folder-button').click();
595	+    await createFolderDialog.waitForOpen();
596	+    await createFolderDialog.createFolder(subfolderName);
597	+    await fileList.waitForItemToAppear(subfolderName, { timeout: 30000 });
598	+
599	+    // 2. Navigate into it (empty-state confirms we are inside the new folder).
600	+    await fileList.doubleClickFolder(subfolderName);
601	+    await page.locator('[data-testid="empty-state"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30000 });
602	+
603	+    // 3. Upload a file into the subfolder.
604	+    const testFile = createTestTextFile(fileName, `restore-after-reload ${runId}`);
605	+    await uploadZone.uploadFile(testFile.path);
606	+    await fileList.waitForItemToAppear(fileName, { timeout: 60000 });
607	+
608	+    // 4. Delete it → moves to the recycle bin (originalParent = subfolder).
609	+    await fileList.rightClickItem(fileName);
610	+    await contextMenu.waitForOpen();
611	+    await contextMenu.clickDelete();
612	+    await confirmDialog.waitForOpen();
613	+    await confirmDialog.clickConfirm();
614	+    await fileList.waitForItemToDisappear(fileName, { timeout: 30000 });
615	+
616	+    // 5. Reload — wipes Zustand store AND the SDK folderTree. Only root is
617	+    //    re-seeded on session restore; the subfolder is NOT loaded.
618	+    await page.reload({ waitUntil: 'domcontentloaded' });
619	+    await page.locator('[data-testid="user-menu"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 120000 }); // session auto-restore
620	+
621	+    // 6. Go straight to the bin WITHOUT navigating into the subfolder, so the
622	+    //    subfolder's keys are absent from folderTree at restore time.
623	+    await binPage.navigate();
624	+    await binPage.waitForBinItem(fileName, { timeout: 30000 });
625	+
626	+    // 7. Restore. Pre-fix this throws 'Target folder not loaded'; post-fix the
627	+    //    SDK self-bootstraps the subfolder from root and succeeds.
628	+    await binPage.restoreItem(fileName);
629	+    await binPage.waitForBinItemToDisappear(fileName, { timeout: 60000 });
630	+
631	+    // 8. Verify the file is restored back into the subfolder.
632	+    await page.getByTestId('nav-item-files').click();
633	+    await page.waitForURL('**/files', { timeout: 15000 });
634	+    await fileList.waitForItemToAppear(subfolderName, { timeout: 30000 });
635	+    await fileList.doubleClickFolder(subfolderName);
636	+    await fileList.waitForItemToAppear(fileName, { timeout: 60000 });
637	+    expect(await fileList.isItemVisible(fileName)).toBe(true);
638	+  });
639	+});

## Simplification review — `ensureFolderLoaded` self-bootstrap diff

Quality-only findings. Each is behavior-preserving.

### `packages/sdk/src/client.ts`

1. **~L451 — redundant `as FolderEntry` cast.** `FolderChild` is a discriminated union (`FolderEntry | FilePointer` on `type`), so `if (child.type !== 'folder') continue;` already narrows `child` to `FolderEntry`. Drop `const entry = child as FolderEntry;` and use `child` directly. Equivalent because TS does the narrowing; the cast adds nothing.

2. **~L431 / ~L455 — `.get(x) ?? null` is noise.** `Map.get` already returns `value | undefined`. The `let root = this.folderTree.get(rootIpnsName) ?? null` and `let childState = this.folderTree.get(entry.ipnsName) ?? null` only coerce `undefined`→`null`; `if (!root)` / `if (!childState)` treat both identically. Simpler: `const root = this.folderTree.get(rootIpnsName);` (and for root, since the only mutation is the reassign-on-miss, the whole block collapses to `const root = this.folderTree.get(rootIpnsName) ?? await this.loadFolder(...); if (!root) return null;`). Behavior identical — the `?? null` never changes a branch outcome.

3. **~L455-456 — `childState` lookup-then-load: the `?? null` + outer `if (!childState)` is the same pattern as #2.** The cache-check `const childState = this.folderTree.get(entry.ipnsName)` is essentially dead in practice: any folder already in `folderTree` is also already in `visited` after its first encounter, and root is pre-seeded into `visited`. The only case it catches is a folder seeded externally (e.g. registerFolder) but not yet visited — worth keeping for that reason, but the `?? null` is still removable. Note `loadFolder` itself calls `this.folderTree.set`, so the load path needs no separate cache write.

4. **~L483-485 — the two post-block guards read awkwardly together.** After the try/catch, `if (!childState) continue;` then `if (entry.ipnsName === targetIpnsName) return childState;` then `stack.push(childState)`. This is fine, but combined with #2/#3 the `!childState` here only fires when `loadFolder` returned null (no IPNS record). That's correct and minimal — flagging only that the comment at L483 ("no IPNS record") is the *sole* remaining trigger, so the earlier `?? null` coercions are confirmed dead weight.

Net for the DFS: the nesting itself (while/for/if-folder/try) is **not** meaningfully reducible without changing behavior — the early-return-on-target and skip-on-failure semantics need the loop shape. The reducible parts are only the `?? null` coercions and the cast (points 1-3).

### Constructor — point 3 in your ask

5. **~L83-86 — conditional spread `...(this.internalRootIpnsKeypair ? { rootIpnsKeypair: ... } : {})` is unnecessary.** Since `internalRootIpnsKeypair` is `... | null` and `config.rootIpnsKeypair?` is optional, you can just assign `rootIpnsKeypair: this.internalRootIpnsKeypair ?? undefined` (or even `?? config.rootIpnsKeypair`, which is already in the `...config` spread). The conditional spread exists only to avoid writing a `null` onto an optional field — but the field is read solely via the `if (!this.internalRootIpnsKeypair)` guard in `ensureFolderLoaded`, which uses the private member, not `config.rootIpnsKeypair`. Simplest: since `...config` already carries the original `config.rootIpnsKeypair`, the entire added spread line is **deletable** — `this.config.rootIpnsKeypair` would just hold the caller's buffer instead of the internal clone, but nothing reads it. If you want `config` to hold the internal clone for consistency with vaultKeypair/rootFolderKey, use the plain `?? undefined` assign. Either is simpler than the conditional spread.

### `packages/sdk/src/__tests__/ensure-folder-loaded.test.ts`

6. **Repeated `new CipherBoxClient(createTestConfig({ rootIpnsKeypair }))` + `mockTree(...)` across 6 of 7 tests.** A small `makeClient()` helper (returns the configured client) would remove the copy-paste. Low value — each line is already one statement — but it's genuine duplication if you want it gone.

7. **L150 test — `unwrapKey` call-count assertion (`toBe(4)`) is brittle over-asserting.** It encodes "2 keys × 2 subfolders" as an implementation detail of the walk. The test's stated purpose ("registering every folder on the path") is already fully covered by the three `hasFolder(...)` asserts above it. The count assertion will break on any internal refactor (e.g. unwrapping both keys in one call) without indicating a real regression. Consider dropping it or asserting `>= 1`.

Tests are otherwise tight — the 7 cases are distinct branches (already-loaded, no-keypair, target-is-root, deep-walk, unreachable, no-IPNS-record, unwrap-failure), no redundant cases.

### Call sites — point 2 in your ask

8. **11× `this.folderTree.get(x) ?? (await this.ensureFolderLoaded(x))` — do NOT collapse, but note the inner `.get` is redundant.** `ensureFolderLoaded` *already* does `const existing = this.folderTree.get(targetIpnsName); if (existing) return existing;` as its first two lines. So every call site's `this.folderTree.get(x) ??` prefix duplicates work the helper does internally. Each site simplifies to `const folder = await this.ensureFolderLoaded(folderIpnsName);` — same behavior (the helper's fast-path returns the cached state), one expression instead of two, and the `?? null` mismatch goes away. This is the highest-value cleanup: it removes the duplicated cache-check from all 11 sites without a new abstraction, keeping each `if (!folder) throw ...` line obvious. (A shared throw-helper is *not* worth it — the error strings differ per site, e.g. 'Parent folder not loaded' / 'Source folder not loaded'.)

### `tests/web-e2e/tests/bin-restore-after-reload.spec.ts`

9. **No redundant waits found.** Each `waitFor`/`waitForItem` gates a distinct state transition (create → navigate → upload → delete → reload → bin → restore → verify). The `Promise.race` for filelist-or-empty-state in `beforeAll` mirrors the existing helper pattern. Nothing to cut.

### Highest-leverage subset

Points **1, 2, 5, 8** are the clean wins (redundant cast, dead `?? null` coercions, conditional-spread, and the duplicated-`.get` at all 11 call sites). Point **8** in particular removes the most repetition with zero new abstraction.

## REUSE Review: `ensureFolderLoaded` self-bootstrap

### 1. DFS walk in `ensureFolderLoaded` — NO genuine reuse fit
`packages/sdk/src/client.ts` ~L444-489. Neither existing tree helper fits, and forcing them would be wrong:
- `collectSubtreeIpnsNames` (client.ts L221) walks **only already-loaded** folders to gather IPNS names for unenroll — it explicitly stops at unloaded subtrees (`if (!folder) return acc`). `ensureFolderLoaded` does the opposite: it *resolves/loads* each unloaded folder via IPNS + key-unwrap as it descends. Different shape, different purpose.
- `sdk-core/src/folder/tree.ts` (`getDepth`/`calculateSubtreeDepth`/`isDescendantOf`) operate on a flat, fully-materialized `Record<string, {id, parentId}>` and walk **upward** via `parentId`. `ensureFolderLoaded` walks **downward** through `children`, lazily materializing nodes that don't exist yet. No overlap.

Verdict: the hand-rolled DFS is justified. No reuse available.

### 2. Defensive keypair copy + per-field `.fill(0)` — minor, matches existing inline pattern
`client.ts` L76-81 (copy), L249-252 (zeroize). There is **no** shared keypair-clone or keypair-zeroize helper in `@cipherbox/crypto` — only `clearBytes(data)` and `clearAll(...buffers)` (`packages/crypto/src/utils/memory.ts`). The new code's `.privateKey.fill(0); .publicKey.fill(0)` exactly mirrors the pre-existing `internalVaultKeypair` block two lines above (L246-247), so it's consistent, not a regression.

Low-value nit: the two zeroize lines could use the already-imported `clearAll(this.internalRootIpnsKeypair.privateKey, this.internalRootIpnsKeypair.publicKey)` — but the surrounding `internalVaultKeypair`/`internalRootFolderKey` code uses raw `.fill(0)` too, so changing only the new block would make it *inconsistent*. Per your "flag only if there's a real shared helper being ignored" instruction: there isn't a keypair helper; `clearAll` exists but isn't used by the established pattern here. Not worth changing in isolation. **Maintenance cost of not reusing: negligible.**

### 3. The `?? (await this.ensureFolderLoaded(x))` pattern — REAL reuse opportunity
Repeated ~11× across throwers: `createFolder` (L511), `renameItem` (L611), `moveItem` (L665-667, twice), `deleteItem` (L741), `uploadFile` (L810), `uploadFiles` (L1005), `replaceFile` (L1280), `restoreFileVersion` (L1393), `deleteFileVersion` (L1477), `shareFolder` (L1854). Each is the same 2-line shape:
```ts
const folder = this.folderTree.get(x) ?? (await this.ensureFolderLoaded(x));
if (!folder) throw new Error('...not loaded');
```
There is **no** existing accessor for this (confirmed: no `requireFolder`/`getFolderOrThrow` in the file). Introduce one concrete private helper:
```ts
private async requireFolder(ipnsName: string, errorMsg = 'Folder not loaded'): Promise<FolderState>
```
returning the state or throwing — collapsing each call site to one line and centralizing the get-or-bootstrap-or-throw contract (note `ensureFolderLoaded` already short-circuits on a cache hit via its own `folderTree.get`, so the outer `?? get()` is even redundant). **Maintenance cost of not reusing:** the throw-message string and the get/bootstrap/null-check ordering are duplicated 11×; any change to bootstrap semantics (e.g. an event on lazy-load, telemetry, a different error type) must be edited in 11 places and is easy to miss — exactly the drift class this codebase keeps hitting. This is the highest-value finding.

### 4. Unwrap of `folderKeyEncrypted` / `ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted` — duplicated, but no helper exists anywhere
`client.ts` L463-470. The exact pair
```ts
unwrapKey(hexToBytes(entry.folderKeyEncrypted), priv)
unwrapKey(hexToBytes(entry.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted), priv)
```
is copy-pasted across the codebase: `apps/web/src/hooks/useFolderNavigation.ts` L233-240, `ShareDialog.tsx` L238/250/308, `useSharedWriteOps.ts` L308, `invite.service.ts` L129, `file-metadata.service.ts` L79, `key-wrapping.ts` L88. There is **no** shared "unwrap a FolderEntry's keys" helper — so the new code duplicates an already-duplicated inline idiom rather than ignoring an existing one. The clean target would be a new helper in `@cipherbox/crypto` or sdk-core (e.g. `unwrapFolderEntryKeys(entry, privateKey) => { folderKey, ipnsPrivateKey }`), but **that's a new abstraction across web + sdk, outside this diff's scope** — flag as a pre-existing duplication this PR adds to, not something it can reuse today. **Maintenance cost:** moderate but ambient; introducing the helper is a separate refactor, don't force it into this PR.

### 5. Test file `ensure-folder-loaded.test.ts` — partially reuses, justified divergence
`packages/sdk/src/__tests__/ensure-folder-loaded.test.ts`. It **does** reuse `createTestConfig` (good; even relies on its `rootIpnsName: 'k51test'` matching `ROOT`). It does **not** reuse `setupFolder` from `helpers.ts`, and that's correct: `setupFolder` seeds a folder with a single **file** child and fixed keys, whereas these tests need **folder** children with specific ipnsNames to drive the DFS — `setupFolder` can't express the multi-level folder trees (`mockTree`, `folderEntry`). The only spot that *could* lean on `setupFolder` is the "already loaded" test (L+inline `folderTree.set`), but it deliberately sets a distinct `sequenceNumber: 5n` to assert no IPNS resolve happened, which `setupFolder`'s `1n` default wouldn't show as clearly. **Verdict: divergence is justified; no meaningful reuse missed.** Minor: the per-test `folderEntry`/`metadata` builders are genuinely new fixtures with no analog in `helpers.ts`, so nothing to consolidate.

---

### Summary (actionable)
- **Worth doing now (in this PR):** Finding #3 — add `private async requireFolder(ipnsName, errorMsg?)` and replace the 11 `?? ensureFolderLoaded` + throw sites. High duplication, zero new abstraction risk, all internal to `client.ts`.
- **Genuinely no fit / leave as-is:** #1 (DFS — both existing walkers are wrong-shaped), #2 (no keypair helper exists; matches established inline pattern), #5 (test divergence justified).
- **Pre-existing duplication, out of scope:** #4 — the FolderEntry key-unwrap idiom is duplicated repo-wide with no helper to call; extracting one is a separate cross-package refactor, not a reuse this diff is ignoring.

## Altitude review: `ensureFolderLoaded` self-bootstrap

### 1. The 11 sprinkled `?? (await this.ensureFolderLoaded(x))` throw sites — SHOULD-FIX-NOW

`packages/sdk/src/client.ts`, ~11 mutation methods (lines 511, 611, 665/667, 741, 810, 1005, 1280, 1393, 1477, 1854).

**Verdict: wrong altitude. This should be ONE chokepoint helper.** The repeated triplet `const x = this.folderTree.get(name) ?? (await this.ensureFolderLoaded(name)); if (!x) throw new Error('Folder not loaded')` is copy-pasted at every site with only the variable name and the throw message varying. That is exactly the failure mode the memory notes describe — "a future method can't forget to add the fallback" (PR #494 dropped 3 such calls precisely because the pattern was manual). Collapse it to a single private resolver:

```ts
private async requireFolder(ipnsName: string, label = 'Folder'): Promise<FolderState> {
  const folder = this.folderTree.get(ipnsName) ?? (await this.ensureFolderLoaded(ipnsName));
  if (!folder) throw new Error(`${label} not loaded`);
  return folder;
}
```

Each call site becomes `const folder = await this.requireFolder(parentIpnsName)` (and `'Parent'`/`'Source'`/`'Destination'`/`'Target'` for the labeled ones). This removes the get-or-load-or-throw decision from 11 hand-maintained sites and makes the fallback structurally impossible to forget. `ensureFolderLoaded` itself stays as the bootstrap engine; `requireFolder` is the chokepoint mutations route through. This is the central fix.

### 2. Bin wrappers seed in the client, use lives in binOps — ACCEPTABLE-FOR-NOW (mild smell)

`packages/sdk/src/client.ts` lines 1674–1693 (`deleteToBin`) and 1715–1735 (`restoreFromBin`); the `folderTree.get` + `'Target folder not loaded'` throw lives in `packages/sdk/src/bin/index.ts` lines 232–233 and 323–324.

The seed (`ensureFolderLoaded`) and the use (`folderTree.get` in `addToBin`/`restoreFromBin`) are split across two layers, which is the same get/throw-without-fallback gap question #1 raises — `binOps` can still throw `'Folder not loaded'` if a future caller forgets to pre-seed. But `binOps` are deliberately pure functions that take `folderTree` as an explicit param and read from stores via parameters (the file header calls this out: "All Zustand store access replaced with explicit parameters"). Passing them a live `ensureLoaded` capability would re-inject client coupling into the layer that was extracted to be capability-free, which is a worse trade. Seeding-in-the-wrapper keeps `binOps` pure. **Leave as-is** — but once `requireFolder` exists (fix #1), the two wrappers should call `await this.requireFolder(folderIpnsName)` / `requireFolder(targetFolderIpnsName, 'Target folder')` instead of bare `ensureFolderLoaded` + relying on binOps to throw, so the throw moves up to the chokepoint and binOps' internal throw becomes a defensive invariant rather than the primary error path.

### 3. `ensureFolderLoaded` as a client method vs. pure DFS in sdk-core — ACCEPTABLE-FOR-NOW

The method is correctly a client method: it needs `internalVaultKeypair`, `internalRootIpnsKeypair`, `config.rootIpnsName`, and `this.loadFolder` (which itself does IPNS resolution + `folderTree.set` + event emission). None of that is pure. The DFS walk is interleaved with side-effecting `loadFolder` calls and unwrap-from-vault-key, so there is no clean pure "DFS-from-children" function to lift into sdk-core without dragging the keypair and the loader along. **Right placement.** Not worth extracting.

### 4. Unwrap logic duplicated in web (`useFolderNavigation.ts`) and SDK (`ensureFolderLoaded`) — ACCEPTABLE-FOR-NOW, FLAG the direction

`apps/web/src/hooks/useFolderNavigation.ts` lines 233–240 and `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` lines 462–472 both do the identical `unwrapKey(hexToBytes(folderEntry.folderKeyEncrypted/ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted), vaultKeypair.privateKey)` pair.

The SDK is the correct home — it owns `folderTree`, the vault keypair, and now the bootstrap walk. The web copy is the bandaid on the way out: the diff's own todo says web `ensureFolderRegistered` calls become redundant once self-bootstrap lands. **This is a real two-sources-of-truth smell** (the unwrap-and-loadFolder sequence now exists in two places that must stay in lockstep — and the SDK-helper-scripts-drift memory shows this codebase has been bitten by exactly this kind of silent divergence). It is a reasonable *intermediate* altitude because the web path is interactive (placeholder insertion, nav-target cancellation, retry-on-IPNS-propagation) and can't be deleted in this PR. **Flag it:** the web's manual unwrap+navigate should eventually call `client.ensureFolderLoaded(ipnsName)` and read back the loaded state, deleting lines 232–293's key handling. Leaving both is fine for now only if a follow-up todo captures the convergence; do not let the two unwrap copies become load-bearing long-term.

### 5. `public` + `@internal` visibility — ACCEPTABLE-FOR-NOW (lean `private`)

`packages/sdk/src/client.ts` line 421. The tests call `client.ensureFolderLoaded(...)` directly (`packages/sdk/src/__tests__/ensure-folder-loaded.test.ts`), so it cannot be `private` without losing direct unit coverage, and `@internal` already strips it from the published API surface (`.d.ts` consumers won't see it). Nothing *outside* the class needs it at runtime — the bin wrappers and mutations are all in-class callers. **`public @internal` is defensible purely to keep the unit test direct.** If you adopt `requireFolder` (fix #1), `ensureFolderLoaded` could become `private` and be tested transitively through `requireFolder`/a mutation — but that's a larger test rewrite, so `public @internal` is the pragmatic call. No change required; just note the only reason it isn't `private` is testability.

---

### Re-entrancy note (not asked, but relevant to fix #1)

`ensureFolderLoaded` calls `this.loadFolder`, which wraps itself in `withOperation`. The 11 mutation sites already run inside their own `withOperation`. So today you get nested `operation:start`/`operation:end` events (e.g. `createFolder` → `loadFolder`) emitted to consumers. That's pre-existing and benign (events, not a lock), but worth knowing: routing everything through `requireFolder` doesn't worsen it, and `withOperation` has no mutex so there's no deadlock risk.

---

### Bottom line

One should-fix-now: **collapse the 11-site get-or-load-or-throw into a single `requireFolder` chokepoint** (#1) — that is the actual altitude defect and it also cleans up the bin wrappers (#2). Everything else (#3 placement, #4 web/SDK unwrap duplication, #5 visibility) is at acceptable intermediate altitude; #4 is the one to leave a tracking todo for so the web copy actually gets deleted rather than living forever.

## Efficiency review: `ensureFolderLoaded` self-bootstrap

All references are to `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/client.ts` unless noted.

### 1. Sequential per-child loads inside the DFS — THEORETICAL win, do NOT parallelize

**Line ~449-487** (the `for (const child of current.children)` loop): each subfolder's two `unwrapKey` calls + one `loadFolder` (network IPNS resolve) are awaited fully before the next sibling starts. Siblings are independent, so naively this looks parallelizable.

**Recommendation: leave it sequential.** The loop has an early-exit (`if (entry.ipnsName === targetIpnsName) return childState`). Parallelizing siblings would forfeit early termination and over-fetch: to reach a target that is the *first* child of root, the sequential path does 1 IPNS resolve + 2 unwraps; a `Promise.all` over root's N children would resolve all N IPNS records and do 2N unwraps before any could short-circuit. Since the common bootstrap target is a single known folder on a shallow path (root → subfolder, depth 1-2 per the bin-restore use case), the sequential early-exit is the *cheaper* strategy for the actual workload. Over-fetching encrypted IPNS records is the more expensive mistake here (network-bound) than the latency of serial awaits on a 1-2-hop path. No change.

The one caveat: this is a blind DFS with no path guidance, so worst-case it can resolve every folder under root before finding a deep target. That's a correctness/scaling property of the design, not waste the diff can trivially remove — a parent-pointer index would fix it but is out of scope for an efficiency cleanup.

### 2. Redundant `folderTree.get` re-resolution — REAL but negligible (correct as written)

**Line ~455:** `let childState = this.folderTree.get(entry.ipnsName) ?? null;` then `loadFolder` (line ~471) internally does `folderTree.set`. This is *not* double work — the `get` is the cache-hit fast path that avoids re-resolving an already-loaded sibling; only on a miss does it call `loadFolder`. Correct.

**Bin double-get — REAL, acceptable, ~tens of ns:** `deleteToBin` (line ~1677) and `restoreFromBin` (line ~1719) call `await this.ensureFolderLoaded(...)` (which does a `folderTree.get`), then delegate to `binOps.addToBin`/`restoreFromBin` which *re-`get`* the same folder (`bin/index.ts:232` and `:323`). So the folder is looked up twice. This is a synchronous `Map.get` — cost is negligible (a handful of ns) and the binOps re-get is unavoidable without changing the binOps signature to accept a pre-resolved `FolderState`. **Verdict: acceptable, not worth changing.** Flag only for awareness.

### 3. Hot path (folder already loaded) — confirmed cheap, but the `await` IS paid on the wrapped mutations

**Line ~276/422** etc., the 11 sites use `this.folderTree.get(x) ?? (await this.ensureFolderLoaded(x))`. Confirmed: `??` short-circuits on the sync left operand (`FolderTree.get` → `Map.get`, `state/folder-tree.ts:24`). When the folder is loaded, the `await` is **never reached** — no microtask, no async hop. Added cost on the hot path for these 11 sites is exactly one extra `Map.get` (the result is then used directly). **Real cost: ~tens of ns/call. Non-issue.** This pattern is correct and the right design.

### 4. `deleteToBin`/`restoreFromBin` unconditional `await ensureFolderLoaded` — REAL minor waste on hot path

**Lines ~1677-1680 and ~1719-1727:** Unlike the 11 `?? (await ...)` sites, these two wrappers call `await this.ensureFolderLoaded(folderIpnsName)` **unconditionally** (statement form, not guarded by `?? `). Even when the folder is already loaded, this:
- enters the async function `ensureFolderLoaded`,
- which returns at its first line (`const existing = this.folderTree.get(...); if (existing) return existing;`),
- but because it's `await`ed, the call still costs one Promise allocation + one microtask-queue round-trip.

**Cheaper alternative:** mirror the other 11 sites — `if (!this.folderTree.has(x)) await this.ensureFolderLoaded(x);` (or the `?? (await ...)` idiom, discarding the result). That keeps the already-loaded case fully synchronous (one `Map.has`), avoiding the Promise + microtask.

**Magnitude: ~1 microtask + 1 Promise alloc per bin op on the hot path (sub-microsecond).** Bin delete/restore are user-driven, low-frequency, network-bound operations, so this is genuinely immaterial — flag as a consistency nit, not a perf fix.

### 5. Closure / captured-scope retention — no concern

The DFS uses a local `stack: FolderState[]` and `visited: Set<string>`. `FolderState` objects in the stack are the *same references* already held by `folderTree` (via `loadFolder`'s `set`), so the stack adds only pointer-sized entries, not duplicated key material — no extra large-buffer retention. Both locals are unreferenced after the method returns and GC normally. The transient unwrapped `folderKey`/`ipnsPrivateKey` buffers (line ~463-470) are scoped to one loop iteration and left to GC (documented, consistent with existing `loadFolder`). The `catch {}` captures nothing. **No closures keep large scopes alive. Clean.**

---

### Summary

| # | Location | Issue | Cheaper alternative | Magnitude / verdict |
|---|----------|-------|---------------------|---------------------|
| 1 | client.ts ~449 (DFS sibling loop) | Serial per-child loads | Keep serial (parallel loses early-exit, over-fetches IPNS) | THEORETICAL — sequential is correct; no change |
| 2a | client.ts ~455 + ~471 | `get` then `loadFolder.set` | none — get is the cache fast-path | Not waste |
| 2b | client.ts ~1677/~1719 vs bin/index.ts:232/323 | Bin folder looked up twice | pass resolved `FolderState` into binOps | REAL but ~ns; acceptable, not worth it |
| 3 | client.ts 11 `?? (await …)` sites | Hot-path cost | n/a — `??` short-circuits sync | ~tens of ns/call; non-issue, well-designed |
| 4 | client.ts ~1678, ~1726 | Unconditional `await ensureFolderLoaded` in bin wrappers | guard with `if (!folderTree.has(x))` | sub-µs (1 Promise + microtask) on hot path; consistency nit |
| 5 | client.ts ~445-487 | Closures/retention | none | Clean — no concern |

**Net: no real wins worth a code change.** The only actionable item is #4 (gate the two bin wrappers' `await` behind a sync `has`/`??` check for consistency with the other 11 sites), and even that is sub-microsecond on a low-frequency, network-bound path — purely a consistency cleanup, not a performance fix. The sequential-DFS-with-early-exit design is the correct efficiency choice for this workload; parallelizing would be a regression.

 packages/sdk/src/client.ts                         | 98 ++++++++++------------

=== requireFolder + root region (post-fix) ===
    // Cannot self-bootstrap without the root IPNS signing key.
    if (!this.internalRootIpnsKeypair) return null;

    // 1. Ensure root is loaded. Root is special: it has no parent to unwrap its
    //    keys from, so they come from config (rootFolderKey + rootIpnsKeypair).
    const rootIpnsName = this.config.rootIpnsName;
    const root =
      this.folderTree.get(rootIpnsName) ??
      (await this.loadFolder(
        rootIpnsName,
        this.internalRootFolderKey,
        this.internalRootIpnsKeypair
      ));
    if (!root) return null;
    if (rootIpnsName === targetIpnsName) return root;

    // 2. DFS from root, unwrapping child keys and loading metadata until the
    //    target is found. `visited` guards against repeats and pathological
    //    cycles in folder metadata.
    const visited = new Set<string>([rootIpnsName]);
    const stack: FolderState[] = [root];
    while (stack.length > 0) {
      const current = stack.pop() as FolderState;
      for (const child of current.children) {
        // type === 'folder' narrows child to FolderEntry (discriminated union).
        if (child.type !== 'folder') continue;
        if (visited.has(child.ipnsName)) continue;
        visited.add(child.ipnsName);

        let childState = this.folderTree.get(child.ipnsName) ?? null;
        if (!childState) {
          try {
            // Unwrap this subfolder's keys with the vault keypair (ECIES), then
            // load its metadata. loadFolder adopts independent clones into the
            // folderTree (zeroed on destroy()); the transient unwrapped buffers
            // are left to GC, matching the existing loadFolder/registerFolder
            // paths which likewise don't eagerly zero their unwrapped inputs.
            const folderKey = await unwrapKey(
              hexToBytes(child.folderKeyEncrypted),
              this.internalVaultKeypair.privateKey
            );
            const ipnsPrivateKey = await unwrapKey(
              hexToBytes(child.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted),
              this.internalVaultKeypair.privateKey
            );
            childState = await this.loadFolder(child.ipnsName, folderKey, {
              // Public key is derived from the private key at signing time.
              publicKey: new Uint8Array(0),
              privateKey: ipnsPrivateKey,
            });
          } catch {
            // A single corrupt/undecryptable sibling entry must not abort the
            // whole bootstrap — skip it so unrelated targets stay reachable.
            // (Generic catch: unwrapKey/hexToBytes throw key-free errors.)
            continue;
          }
        }
        // Could not resolve this subfolder (no IPNS record) — skip it.
        if (!childState) continue;
        if (child.ipnsName === targetIpnsName) return childState;
        stack.push(childState);
      }
    }

    // Target not found anywhere under root.
    return null;
  }

  /**
   * Resolve a folder from internal state, self-bootstrapping from root if needed.
   *
   * Returns the loaded FolderState or throws `${label} not loaded`. This is the
   * single chokepoint every folderTree-dependent mutation routes through, so the
   * get-or-self-load-or-throw contract lives in one place and a new method can't
   * silently forget the self-heal fallback.
   *
   * @param ipnsName - IPNS name of the required folder
   * @param label - Human label for the error (e.g. 'Parent folder', 'Source folder')
   */
  private async requireFolder(ipnsName: string, label = 'Folder'): Promise<FolderState> {
    const folder = this.folderTree.get(ipnsName) ?? (await this.ensureFolderLoaded(ipnsName));
```

## Summary

`/simplify` ran 4 parallel cleanup agents (reuse / simplification / efficiency / altitude) over the diff. They converged on one high-value fix plus a few small ones — all applied, behavior-preserving:

| Cleanup | What changed |
|---|---|
| **`requireFolder` chokepoint** | Collapsed the 11 duplicated `get(x) ?? ensureFolderLoaded(x); if (!folder) throw` blocks (+ 2 bin wrappers) into one `private requireFolder(ipnsName, label)`. Flagged independently by 3 of 4 agents as the #1 fix — a new method can no longer silently drop the self-heal fallback (the exact #494 drift class). Net −9 lines in `client.ts`. |
| **Redundant cast** | Dropped `as FolderEntry` — the `type === 'folder'` guard already narrows the discriminated union. |
| **Root load** | One `?? await loadFolder` expression instead of `let` + `if`-reassign + `?? null`. |
| **Constructor** | `rootIpnsKeypair: … ?? undefined` instead of a conditional spread. |
| **Test** | Removed a brittle `unwrapKey` call-count assertion; the `hasFolder` asserts already cover path registration. |

**Skipped (with reason):** DFS parallelization (loses early-exit, over-fetches IPNS — correctly advised against); extracting a shared key-unwrap helper / sdk-core DFS (cross-package, out of scope); `clearAll` for zeroize (would diverge from the existing inline pattern); making `ensureFolderLoaded` private (kept `public @internal` for direct unit-test access).

**Verification after cleanup:** `pnpm typecheck` clean (full chain + web `tsc -b`), **179 SDK tests pass** (3 live-API skipped under CI), ESLint clean, pre-commit lint-staged passed.

Branch `feat/sdk-client-self-bootstrap-folder-tree` is pushed with both commits (`feat` + `refactor`). No PR opened yet — say the word and I'll open it. One genuine follow-up the altitude reviewer flagged: the web's `useFolderNavigation` unwrap path is now a duplicate of the SDK's and the ~16 web `ensureFolderRegistered` calls are redundant — both can be deleted in a follow-up once self-heal proves out.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/security/REVIEW-20260616-025021.md",
  "content": "# Security Review Report\n\n**Date:** 2026-06-16 02:50:21\n**Scope:** Branch `feat/sdk-client-self-bootstrap-folder-tree` (`898fbdc51..eec7f8686`) — SDK client self-bootstrap of the folder tree from the root IPNS key\n**Reviewer:** Claude (security:review command), corroborated by an independent `security-reviewer` subagent pass on the same diff\n\n## Executive Summary\n\nThe change lets `CipherBoxClient` self-bootstrap its `folderTree` by walking from root and unwrapping each subfolder's ECIES-wrapped keys with the vault keypair, instead of requiring the web app to pre-seed every folder. It adds an optional `rootIpnsKeypair` to the client config (defensively copied + zeroed on `destroy()`). It introduces **no new cryptographic primitives** — no new encryption, IV/nonce generation, KDF, or randomness. It only invokes the existing `unwrapKey` (ECIES) and `loadFolder` (AES-256-GCM metadata decrypt, unchanged in sdk-core). Key material handling is consistent with the established `vaultKeypair`/`rootFolderKey` pattern, and no keys are logged, persisted, or sent to the server.\n\n**Risk Level:** LOW\n\n## Files Reviewed\n\n| File | Crypto Operations | Risk Level |\n|------|-------------------|------------|\n| `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` | ECIES unwrap of `folderKeyEncrypted` / `ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted`; key-material defensive copy + zeroization; key passing into `folderTree` | LOW |\n| `packages/sdk/src/types.ts` | New `rootIpnsKeypair` config field (Ed25519 keypair, in-memory only) | LOW |\n| `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts` | Passes `vaultStore.rootIpnsKeypair` into the SDK config (client-side, no transmission) | LOW |\n| `packages/sdk/src/__tests__/ensure-folder-loaded.test.ts` | Test only (mocks crypto) | N/A |\n| `tests/web-e2e/tests/bin-restore-after-reload.spec.ts` | Test only | N/A |\n\n## Findings\n\n### Critical Issues\n\nNone.\n\n### High Priority\n\nNone.\n\n### Medium Priority\n\n1. **DFS amplification on unreachable / withheld target (availability, not key compromise)**\n   - **Severity:** MEDIUM\n   - **Location:** `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` — `ensureFolderLoaded` DFS loop (~lines 445–490)\n   - **Description:** When the target IPNS name is not reachable from root (stale reference, not-yet-propagated IPNS record, or a record the untrusted relay withholds), the walk exhausts the entire reachable tree — N IPNS resolves + 2N ECIES unwraps — and there is no negative cache, so a caller that tight-loops on an unresolvable target repeats the full walk each attempt.\n   - **Impact:** Self-inflicted CPU/network amplification under retry; an untrusted server can force maximum work by withholding the target's record. Bounded by vault size. No key compromise and no zero-knowledge violation.\n   - **Recommendation:** Acceptable to ship — the real callers (bin restore, mutations) target *reachable* folders and short-circuit on the first hit. If upstream retry loops are added later, add a short-lived per-target negative memo or a walked-folder cap. Tracked as a follow-up, not a blocker.\n   - **Reference:** Defensive-availability hardening; CWE-834 (excessive iteration) in the pathological case only.\n\n### Low Priority / Recommendations\n\n1. **Transient unwrapped key buffers left to GC during a full walk**\n   - **Severity:** LOW\n   - **Location:** `client.ts` ~lines 313–325\n   - **Description:** Each visited subfolder's unwrapped `folderKey`/`ipnsPrivateKey` are cloned into `folderTree` (zeroed on `destroy()`) and the originals are left to GC — the same hygiene as the pre-existing `loadFolder`/`registerFolder` paths. A *full* walk holds more simultaneously-live plaintext than a single load, but it is the same *class*, not a new leak. Eager zeroing is infeasible: `loadFolder` returns the target `FolderState` by reference and the caller publishes with those buffers, so zeroing them would corrupt the result.\n   - **Recommendation:** No change. Documented in-code.\n\n2. **`getConfig()` exposes `rootIpnsKeypair` by reference**\n   - **Severity:** LOW / INFO\n   - **Location:** `client.ts` `getConfig()` (pre-existing method)\n   - **Description:** `getConfig()` returns `this.config` directly; `rootIpnsKeypair` now aliases the internal copy, so a consumer could read/mutate the root signing key. This is identical to the existing exposure of `vaultKeypair`/`rootFolderKey` — consistent, not a regression. `getConfig` is `@internal`.\n   - **Recommendation:** If hardening `getConfig` later, return a key-free shallow copy for all three fields together. Out of scope for this PR.\n\n## Detailed Analysis\n\n### `CipherBoxClient.ensureFolderLoaded` + `requireFolder` (`packages/sdk/src/client.ts`)\n\n**What it does:** Resolves a folder into `folderTree`, self-bootstrapping from root if absent. DFS-walks from root; for each subfolder child it ECIES-unwraps `folderKeyEncrypted` → AES folder key and `ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted` → Ed25519 signing key with the vault private key, loads metadata, and caches the path. `requireFolder` is the single get-or-self-load-or-throw chokepoint all 11 mutations + 2 bin wrappers route through.\n\n**Crypto operations:**\n- `unwrapKey(hexToBytes(child.folderKeyEncrypted), vaultPrivateKey)` — ECIES (secp256k1) decrypt\n- `unwrapKey(hexToBytes(child.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted), vaultPrivateKey)` — ECIES decrypt\n- `loadFolder(...)` → `sdkCore.loadFolderMetadata` — AES-256-GCM metadata decrypt (unchanged)\n\n**Issues found:** See MEDIUM (amplification) and LOW (transient buffers) above.\n\n**Positive observations:**\n- **No key transposition / confused deputy:** `folderKeyEncrypted` → `folderKey` arg, `ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted` → `privateKey` keypair field. Each child's own encrypted fields are unwrapped fresh; a parent's key is never reused for a child.\n- **Recipient key correct:** both unwraps use `internalVaultKeypair.privateKey` (the secp256k1 ECIES key the subfolder keys are wrapped to), not the Ed25519 IPNS key.\n- **No error leakage:** `unwrapKey` throws a generic key-free `CryptoError`; `hexToBytes` throws `'Invalid hex string'`. The new `catch {}` swallows per-sibling failures without logging and `continue`s, so one corrupt entry can't abort the whole bootstrap or leak bytes.\n- **Cycles bounded:** `visited` Set is populated before any unwrap/load, so each IPNS name is resolved at most once; cycles terminate.\n- **Backward compatible & fail-safe:** with no `rootIpnsKeypair`, `ensureFolderLoaded` returns `null` *before any crypto*, preserving the prior `'Folder not loaded'` error.\n\n### `rootIpnsKeypair` config + lifecycle (`types.ts`, `client.ts` constructor/`destroy`)\n\n**Crypto operations:** Defensive deep-copy of the Ed25519 keypair into `internalRootIpnsKeypair`; per-field `.fill(0)` in `destroy()`.\n\n**Issues found:** None.\n\n**Positive observations:**\n- Mirrors the `internalVaultKeypair`/`internalRootFolderKey` pattern exactly: caller buffers are never zeroed (only internal copies are), `destroy()` zeroes both `publicKey` and `privateKey`, and the `folderTree` clone of the root keypair is zeroed by `folderTree.clear()`. No double-free, no caller-buffer corruption, no un-zeroed internal copy.\n\n### Web wiring (`apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts`)\n\n**What it does:** Passes `vaultState.rootIpnsKeypair` (already in the in-memory Zustand `vaultStore`, zeroed on logout) into `initSdkClient`. Guaranteed non-null by the existing `if (vaultState.rootFolderKey && vaultState.rootIpnsKeypair && vaultState.rootIpnsName)` guard.\n\n**Issues found:** None. No new persistence, no transmission. The root IPNS key already lived client-side in `vaultStore`; this only hands a defensive copy to the SDK that already holds equivalent key material.\n\n## Test Cases\n\n### `ensureFolderLoaded` — existing coverage (`ensure-folder-loaded.test.ts`, 7 cases)\n\nAlready implemented: already-loaded no-op (no IPNS resolve), no-keypair returns null before crypto, root-as-target, deep multi-level walk + path caching, unreachable-target returns null, subfolder with no IPNS record skipped, **sibling whose key unwrap throws is skipped and target still reached**.\n\n### Suggested additional cases (gaps)\n\n#### Unit Tests\n\n```typescript\ndescribe('ensureFolderLoaded — security edges', () => {\n  // Negative: corrupt hex in a child entry must not abort the whole walk\n  it('skips a child with malformed *Encrypted hex and still reaches the target', async () => {\n    // hexToBytes throws on odd-length hex -> caught -> continue (already wired);\n    // assert target resolves and the bad sibling is NOT registered.\n  });\n\n  // Cycle: A lists B, B lists A — must terminate, not infinite-loop\n  it('terminates on a cyclic folder graph without re-resolving a visited folder', async () => {\n    // mockTree({ root:[A], k51A:[B], k51B:[A] }); ensureFolderLoaded('k51missing')\n    // assert loadFolderMetadata called once per distinct ipnsName, returns null.\n  });\n\n  // Zeroization: destroy() zeroes the root IPNS keypair copy, not the caller's\n  it('destroy() zeroes internalRootIpnsKeypair without mutating the caller buffer', () => {\n    const kp = { publicKey: new Uint8Array(32).fill(7), privateKey: new Uint8Array(64).fill(8) };\n    const client = new CipherBoxClient(createTestConfig({ rootIpnsKeypair: kp }));\n    client.destroy();\n    expect(kp.privateKey.every((b) => b === 8)).toBe(true); // caller copy untouched\n  });\n\n  // Wrong-key isolation: a child wrapped to a different vault key fails to unwrap\n  // and is skipped, not registered under a wrong/empty key.\n  it('skips a child whose keys were wrapped to a different vault key', async () => {\n    // unwrapKey rejects (CryptoError) for that child -> caught -> continue.\n  });\n});\n```\n\n#### Integration / E2E\n\n- [x] Restore a bin item whose subfolder parent was never navigated to, after a page reload (`bin-restore-after-reload.spec.ts`) — exercises the real ECIES-unwrap-from-root path.\n- [ ] Mutation (rename/upload/move) into a never-navigated subfolder after reload — confirms `requireFolder` self-heals there too (currently only bin restore is E2E-covered).\n\n#### Attack Scenarios to Test\n\n- [ ] **Server withholds a subfolder's IPNS record** — `loadFolderMetadata` returns null; assert the walk skips it and does not hang or throw a non-generic error (covered by unit \"no IPNS record\" case; add a withholding-relay integration variant).\n- [ ] **Tampered wrapped key** (flip a byte of `folderKeyEncrypted`) — ECIES auth fails → `CryptoError` → child skipped; assert no plaintext/partial key surfaces in the thrown error.\n- [ ] **Key-confusion** — a child entry that swaps `folderKeyEncrypted` and `ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted` values; assert decrypt/usage fails closed (skipped) rather than registering a folder with a mismatched key.\n\n## Compliance Checklist\n\n- [x] No `privateKey` in localStorage/sessionStorage — diff adds none; root key stays in in-memory Zustand `vaultStore`.\n- [x] No sensitive keys logged — no `console.*`/error-with-key added; `catch {}` is silent.\n- [x] No unencrypted keys sent to server — diff performs no network key transmission; keys only unwrapped locally.\n- [x] ECIES used for key wrapping — subfolder keys unwrapped via `unwrapKey` (ECIES secp256k1).\n- [x] AES-256-GCM used for content encryption — metadata decrypt path (sdk-core) unchanged.\n- [x] Server has zero knowledge of plaintext — unchanged; all unwrap/decrypt is client-side.\n- [x] IPNS keys encrypted with TEE public key before republish — unaffected; this change only *reads/unwraps* IPNS keys client-side for local signing, not the TEE republish path.\n\n## Recommendations Summary\n\n| Priority | Recommendation | Effort |\n|----------|----------------|--------|\n| P2 | Add a per-target negative memo / walk cap **iff** upstream adds retry loops on unresolvable targets | MEDIUM |\n| P2 | Add unit cases: cycle termination, destroy() zeroization, wrong-key skip (suggested above) | LOW |\n| P2 | E2E: a *mutation* (not just bin restore) into a never-navigated subfolder after reload | LOW |\n| P3 | Consider a key-free `getConfig()` return for all three key fields together (pre-existing exposure) | LOW |\n\n## Next Steps\n\n1. **Immediate:** None required — no critical/high findings; safe to open the PR.\n2. **Short-term:** Add the 3 suggested unit cases (cycle, zeroization, wrong-key) to harden coverage.\n3. **Long-term:** If retry-on-unresolvable patterns appear in the web/FUSE tier, add the negative cache; revisit the web↔SDK unwrap duplication (delete the redundant web `ensureFolderRegistered` path).\n\n---\n*Generated by security:review command*\n*This review is automated guidance, not a substitute for a professional security audit*\n"
}

**Scope:** branch `feat/sdk-client-self-bootstrap-folder-tree` (`898fbdc51..eec7f8686`)
**Risk Level:** **LOW**

| Severity | Count |
|----------|-------|
| Critical | 0 |
| High | 0 |
| Medium | 1 |
| Low / Info | 2 |

**Key conclusion:** the diff introduces **no new crypto primitives** — no encryption, IV/nonce, KDF, or randomness. It only ECIES-*unwraps* existing keys and loads metadata via the unchanged sdk-core AES-256-GCM path. All 7 compliance rules pass: no key logging, no new storage, no server transmission, correct key→field mapping (no transposition), correct ECIES recipient key, fail-closed on bad keys, `destroy()` zeroization consistent with the existing pattern.

**The 3 findings (none blocking):**
1. **[MEDIUM]** DFS amplification on an *unreachable/withheld* target (availability, not key compromise) — real callers target reachable folders and short-circuit; only bites under retry loops on bad targets. Follow-up, not a blocker.
2. **[LOW]** Transient unwrapped buffers left to GC during a full walk — same class as existing `loadFolder`; eager zeroing is infeasible (target returned by reference).
3. **[INFO]** `getConfig()` exposes `rootIpnsKeypair` by reference — pre-existing pattern (same as `vaultKeypair`).

**Test cases:** 7 already implemented in `ensure-folder-loaded.test.ts`; the report suggests 3 new hardening unit cases (cycle termination, `destroy()` zeroization, wrong-key skip) + an attack-scenario checklist (tampered wrapped key, key-confusion, record withholding).

**Full report:** `.planning/security/REVIEW-20260616-025021.md`

This matches the earlier independent `security-reviewer` pass (0 critical/high) and CodeRabbit (0 findings) — the feature is clean to ship.

So the client could not resolve/publish root or lazy-load folders on its own — the web app had to pre-seed `folderTree` via `ensureFolderRegistered` before every folderTree-dependent method.
